Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
Overall September ODM NB shipments came in 2% below MSe: Quarter-end pull-
ins drove notebook shipments up 32% m/m, although volumes were 2% below MSe,
with weakness mainly coming from Wistron and Compal, and to a lesser extent,
Pegatron, reflecting slower NB shipments from Dell, as Dell accounted for ~60% of

Compal's NB shipment, and ~55% of Wistron's in CY25, on our estimates. Thus total
3Q NB builds came in at 25.2M units (-17% q/q, -26% y/y). This sequential decline
is sub-seasonal vs. the 15Y average seasonality of +5% q/q in 3Q.
Pull-forward demand appears to have come to an end, channel partners and
distributors have stocked up on inventory, and OEMs are slowing their sales into the
channel. We believe this is why our conversations with the ODMs suggest a slightly

weaker 4Q volume outlook than previously expected, despite favorable mix shift.
Thus, we lower our 4Q NB build estimate by 3% to 23.1M (-8% q/q, -29% y/y).
This sequential decline is sub-seasonal (despite an already lowered base in 3Q) vs.
the 15Y average seasonality of flat q/q in 4Q. We forecast October NB builds at
6.8M (-30% m/m, -33% y/y). Our revised forecasts imply CY26 builds of ~107.8M
units (-16% y/y) for the top five Taiwan NB ODMs.

Key Takeaways
Top five ODMs' September 2026 NB shipments were 9.7M units (+32% m/m, -
20% y/y), 2% below our estimate.
Total 3Q26 NB builds came in at 25.2M (-17% q/q, -26% y/y).
We see October 2026 NB builds at 6.8M units (-30% m/m, -33% y/y).
We lower our 4Q26 NB build estimate by 3% to 23.1M units (-8% q/q, -29% y/y).
Our top five ODMs' CY26 NB build estimate is ~107.8M units (-16% y/y).

October 14, 2026 09:00 PM GMT

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Allen Chang

Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C. 
Verena Jeng 
+852-2978-1681 | verena.jeng@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C.
AVC (3017.TW): Liquid cooling rising penetration and AI servers ramp-up 
to drive growth ahead; Buy
19 October 2026 | 3:24AM HKT
3-month revenues preview: We expect AVC’s monthly revenues to continue 
sequential growth through Dec 2026, leading 4Q26 revenues to grow at +42% YoY/ 
+15% QoQ, driven by: (1) new AI server racks ramp-up, driving the overall liquid 
cooling penetration rate. As we highlighted in Oct (report link), we expect AI server 
rack (NVL72-equivalent) shipment (incl. Nvidia and AMD) to reach 63k/ 110k/ 164k 
in 2026-28E, reflecting our positive view on AI server racks. (2) Its major ASIC AI 
server customer’s new model ramp-up, driving its air-cooling and liquid-cooling 
components, along with a rising liquid cooling penetration rate. (3) Its customer base 
expansion to a new ASIC AI server, to further drive its liquid cooling components in 
4Q26. And (4) increasing adoption rate of ultra-thin VC in consumer electronics to fit 
the lightweight design and major customer’s foldable phone model. We expect AVC’s 
net income to grow at a +39% CAGR in 2026-28E, riding on the strong AI spending, a 
growing liquid cooling penetration rate, and AVC’s leading market position in total 
solutions (air cooling, liquid cooling with various components / systems). Maintain 
Buy.

### Page 2

Earnings revisions: We factor in AVC’s Sep revenue and revise down our 2026E net 
income by -1%, mainly on lower revenues in 4Q26E, reflecting the slow consumer 
electronics under high memory costs and AI servers model transition. We revise up our 
2027-28E net income by 4% / 0%, mainly on higher revenues and GMs. Our 2027-28E 
revenue is revised up by 1% / 1%, mainly on a higher contribution from AI server liquid 
cooling components. 2027-28E GM are revised up to reflect the rising liquid cooling 
components driving profitability.

Valuation: We continue to use a near-term P/E to derive our 12-month target price and 
update our target P/E multiple to 30.6x 2027E EPS (vs. 30.3x previously), which is still 
derived from PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E multiple correlation. Our target P/E 
multiple is above AVC’s historical average +1stv. forward trading P/E, reflecting our 
positive view on AVC riding on the rising penetration rate of liquid-cooled AI servers. 
With our updated target P/E and earnings estimates, we raise our 12-month target price 
to NT$4,736 (from NT$4,513). Maintain Buy.

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology - AVC 
We are Buy-rated on AVC. Our 12-month target price for AVC is based on a near-term 
P/E, consistent with our Taiwan Technology coverage. We base our target price of 
NT$4,736 on a target P/E multiple of 30.6x applied to our forward-year EPS (2027E). 
Our target P/E is derived from the correlation between P/E and EPS growth of its peers 
in technology hardware.  
Key downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected adoption of liquid cooling; (2) 
lower-than-expected demand for AI servers; (3) fiercer-than-expected competition; 
and (4) a slower-than-expected recovery in general servers.

Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

雙鴻為散熱解決方案供應商 
 雙鴻為散熱解決方案供應商 
雙鴻成立於 1998 年 8 月 ， 為國內前二大NB 散熱模組廠 ， 過去主要生產散熱器 、
散熱片、散熱模組、散熱導管、風扇等，現在已轉型為提供客戶完整散熱解決
方案供應商。主要應用於筆記型電腦、桌上型電腦、智慧型手機及伺服器等領
域。2Q26 產品應用領域 Server 78%、PC 17%、VGA/Gaming 4%、及Others(手
機及車用) 1%。另外水冷業務比重於 1H26 約為 55~60%。

 2Q26 毛利率 28.4%，低於預期，2Q26 EPS 10.41 元 
雙鴻 2Q26 EPS 10.41 元，低於元富預估的 13.72 元，獲利達成率 76%。本業
來看，2Q26 營收 87 億元，+2%QOQ，+64%YOY，2Q26 毛利率 28.42%，較
1Q26 衰退 1.2 個百分點 ， 亦低於預估的30.5%， 主要是報廢品打消 ， 預期3Q26
毛利率回復上 1Q26 水準，2Q26 EPS 10.41 元。

雙鴻 3Q26 營收 97.91 億元，+12.5%QOQ，+64.3%YOY，營收達成率 94%，
低於預期，主要是七月營收有遞延認列，故推估基期過高，實際 3Q26 拉貨力
道未如預期顯著，預估 3Q26 毛利率 30%，預估 3Q26 EPS 14.13 元。

營運逐季成長，氣冷及水冷兩塊
業務均樂觀

營運逐季成長，氣冷及水冷兩塊業務均樂觀 
2025 年雙鴻水冷營收比重 35%，1Q26 提升至 55%，帶動公司業績大幅增長。
氣冷部分，2024 年月營收 2-2.5 億元，2025 年 4～4.5 億元，2026 年平均 6～
7 億元，氣冷水冷均樂觀，以 AWS TR3 來看，氣冷及水冷比重至少是 70~80%
對比 20~30%，TR4 將有機會為 100%全水冷 ， 水冷大趨勢不變 ，VR 9 月起量，
TR3 9 月起量 Compute tray 及 Switch tray 及 Rack manifolds 都有相關產品出
貨，Compute tray 水冷產值 3000usd 以上，Switch tray 水冷產值 1000usd 以
上，ASIC 水冷產值高於 GPU 客戶，2H26~2027 年 ASIC 增長可期。

1H26 GB 水冷營收中 Rack manifolds 佔比 30-35%、Cold Plate+inner manifolds
佔比 55-60%、CDU 5% ，2026 年可視為 CDU 元年，2027 年營收跳增可期。
公司展望 3Q26 營收+15~20%QOQ，實際+12.5%QOQ 低於預期，4Q26 營收
QOQ+雙位數，2026 年營收+70~85%YOY，3Q26 毛利率將回到 29.7%以上水
準，2027 年維持正向看法。

維持買進，目標價 1800 元

維持買進評等，目標價 1800 元 
2Q26 毛利率低於預期為一次性因素，氣冷及水冷兩塊業務樂觀，水冷擴張趨勢
明確，故維持買進，預估2026 年 EPS 55.74 元，預估 2027 年 EPS 90.36 元，
目標價 1800 元。(2027 PER 20x)

風險提示 
出貨不如預期，ASP 不如預期。

### Page 5

5
Gogolook三大事業體
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整
⚫ Gogolook 是全球領先的信任科技 (TrustTech) 公司，運用自有防詐資料庫與 AI 技
術，提供消費者防詐、企業端防詐與金融科技服務。Gogolook 擁有全球最齊全的
通訊與數位防詐數據資料庫，涵蓋電話號碼、網址、和虛擬貨幣錢包地址等。

### Page 6

6
whoscall
⚫ whoscall 為 Gogolook 所
開發出的個人數位防詐
APP，全球下載量已突破
1億次，月活躍用戶超過
1,800萬，主要服務市場
涵蓋台灣、泰國、日本、
韓國、香港、巴西、馬
來西亞、菲律賓等。
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整

### Page 7

7
whoscall海外市場持續擴張
⚫ whoscall海外市場擴張成效卓越，海外營收占比於2025年已突破63%。泰國為
Gogolook最大的海外市場，其用戶規模、營收貢獻已超越台灣，但仍具備很大的
成長空間；公司於曼谷擁有龐大用戶基礎，目前正進軍泰國二、三線城市。菲律
賓與馬來西亞等地已具備百萬等級的活躍用戶，後續成長可期。
⚫ Gogolook於09/2026已正式和泰國政府退休基金(Government Pension Fund，GPF)簽
署MOU，並與國家跨行交易管理與交換公司(National ITMX)及泰國存款保障機構
(Deposit Protection Agency，DPA)合作，將whoscall防詐服務導入GPF體系，涵蓋
退休人員及現職公務員(超過120萬名會員)。此合作有助於同步推廣泰國當地B2C、
B2B業務。
⚫ 為了有效控制獲客成本，Gogolook在海外減少依賴成效較低的數位廣告，轉而與
當地政府、銀行及電信商合作。此外，考量到東南亞信用卡普及率較低，

Gogolook在泰國率先推出的「電信帳單扣款」訂閱模式大獲成功，目前此模式也
與台灣遠傳電信合作，藉此攻佔中高年齡層客群。

### Page 8

8
whoscall海外市場持續擴張
⚫ 針對品牌偏好較強或市場規模較小的國家(如日本與新加坡)，則透過白牌授權策略，
協助當地業者開發自有防詐App並從中分潤。
⚫ whoscall於02/2026~05/2026與日本Rakuten Mobile進行合作，推出「騷擾電話・簡
訊防護」活動，提供申請用戶免費試用，目前開始有試用用戶轉為付費訂閱，有
望挹注2H26日本市場消費者防詐業務成長動能。
資料來源：群益投顧彙整

### Page 9

9
whoscall海外市場持續擴張
資料來源：群益投顧彙整
穩定貢獻營收
貼牌合作、自有
品牌雙軸並進。
貼牌合作策略。
曼谷已站穩根基，
目前進軍二、三線
城市，並擴大與泰
國政府合作。主要
成長動能來源。
已具備百萬用戶

### Page 10

10
Gogolook收購ScamAdviser
⚫ 企業端防詐方面，由於各國法規有加重企業防詐責任的趨勢，Gogolook將此業務
視為長期成長動能潛力來源。Gogolook於2024年收購具備超過6,000萬筆網域資料
庫的ScamAdviser後，成功拓展歐美市場。儘管2025年因主動調整部分低毛利率業
務，導致ScamAdviser營收下滑，但其實質獲利反而增加。
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整
⚫ ScamAdviser成立於荷蘭阿姆斯
特丹，為全球防詐聯盟GASA
基石成員。ScamAdviser以AI演
算法與資料庫為基礎，開發自
有的網站風險評級技術，與
Gogolook的企業防詐解決方案
與電話號碼資料庫整合，為全
球最完整的通訊與數位防詐數
據資料庫。

### Page 11

11
Gogolook企業端防詐服務
⚫ 企業端服務主要分為「資料庫授權」與「商譽保護」兩大主軸：(1) 防詐情資方案，
為一種資料庫授權模式，將whoscall的「電話/簡訊」資料庫以及ScamAdviser的
「網域」資料庫，透過API、SDK方式授權給企業。(2) 商譽保護服務，企業的外
部防詐監控中心，協助企業或政府主動在網路上巡邏，偵測並下架冒用其品牌的
假投資廣告、假粉專或釣魚網站。
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整

### Page 12

12
Agent looker
⚫ 因應Agentic AI時代來臨，Gogolook於09/2026推出新產品Agent looker，工作原理
是在AI agent實際造訪或讀取網頁前，即時進行風險檢測並攔截惡意請求，可有效
避免AI agent被詐騙的風險。
⚫ 核心防護機制與功能：(1)毫秒級即時掃描，當AI Agent讀取外部網頁或執行URL
請求時，Agent Looker會在毫秒內對網頁封包與內容進行深度檢查，攔截惡意腳本、
釣魚網站以及針對 AI 模型的提示詞注入(Prompt Injection)攻擊。(2)Gogolook威脅
情資資料庫驅動。系統底層串接 Gogolook 累積多年的全球防詐騙、釣魚網站與風
險網域黑名單。基於海量真實風險樣本的數據庫，使其能快速且精準地辨識異常
行為。(3)活動稽核日誌。提供視覺化的管理儀表板，完整記錄AI Agent的所有外
部網路互動軌跡，包含造訪過的網址、讀取的網頁片段及最終的安全判定結果，
確保AI運作透明且可追溯。
⚫ Agent looker目前處於封閉測試(Private Beta)階段。

資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整

### Page 13

13
金融科技-袋鼠金融
⚫ 金融科技為Gogolook於疫情期間因應線上詐騙激增而開創的新業務，包含全台灣
流量最大的金融媒合平台「袋鼠金融Roo.Cash」，以及專攻小額借款的「JUJI招財
麻吉」。袋鼠金融 Roo.Cash與國內金融機構合作，提供200件以上的金融商品方案
智慧比較與媒合。智能問答服務「Roo AI」，可協助用戶在信用卡、貸款、保險、
投資理財與民生優惠資訊中，快速找到最適合的選項。若順利成交，Gogolook即
可藉此獲得媒合收入。
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整

### Page 14

14
金融科技-JUJI招財麻吉
⚫ JUJI招財麻吉鎖定10萬元以下、傳統銀行不願承作的利基市場，自行舉辦機車貸
款、手機貸款等業務。該業務最核心的優勢在於將whoscall的通話行為數據轉化為
AI風控模型，不僅可嚴格控制核貸率，更將90天延滯率壓在極低水準。目前JUJI
招財麻吉應收分期款餘額持續增加，公司同時也把純風控模型顧問服務輸出至海
外市場，進一步擴大變現能力，2Q26已成功在泰國市場簽訂首批企業訂單。
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整

### Page 15

15
Gogolook營運策略
資料來源：群益投顧彙整

### Page 16

16
Gogolook營收續增，毛利率維持高檔
⚫ 隨著消費者防詐、企業端防詐、金融科技三大事業皆穩定增長，預估營收呈現穩
定增長趨勢。觀察重點：whoscall於各國推展進度、企業對於agent looker接受度、
招財麻吉貸放額度及壞帳率變化、AI風控模型專案。
⚫ 毛利率有望穩定落於88~90%。
資料來源：群益投顧彙整

### Page 17

17
Gogolook營業費用控管得宜
⚫ 低成本合作取量：減少高成本數位廣告，擴大與政府單位、電信商、銀行合作，
低成本觸及海外客戶。
⚫ AI優化營運效率：利用AI提升工程與內部效率，人力控管但產出持續提升。
⚫ 費用率逐步下滑：隨著營收規模提升，費用控管得宜，2024年營業費用率96.78%，
2025年營業費用率82.29%，預估2026年營業費用率69.21%。
資料來源：群益投顧彙整

### Page 18

18
營運突破關鍵規模，獲利顯著增長
資料來源：群益投顧彙整
⚫ 由於毛利率接近90%，營業費用控管得宜，隨著營收於4Q25突破關鍵規模點後，
獲利表現開始明顯跳升。由於2026年獲利表現優於預期，過往累積虧損大致抵減
完畢，2027年稅率將回升至常態水準，但可有望開始發放現金股利。

本次報告：公司拜訪

南電 (8046 TT) 
ABF 報價優於預期，獲利再度上修

永豐觀點 
南電 ABF 報價漲幅優於預期，2027 年獲利翻倍上修，長線 ABF 供給吃緊至 2028
年態勢不變，獲利延續性可期。 
 投資評價與建議 
南電 3Q26 營收達成率 125%，暗示 ABF 報價漲幅優於預期，長線GPU、ASIC 以
及 CPU 在 AI 需求推升下趨勢向上，南電因報價以短約為主於漲價週期更能快速
反應獲利，研究部上調 2026-2028 年稅後 EPS 至 18.90 元、50.93 元以及 76.51
元，投資建議維持買進，目標價反映獲利上調至 1660 元(26X2027-2028 年平均
EPS)。

ESG 評析 
南電企業永續評鑑整體為 A 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 A 等與 A 等。

南電為專業 ABF 製造商 ：南電為國內專業 IC 載板製造商 ，1H26 產品別 PC(17%)、
Networking(49%)、Consumer(12%)、Auto(6%)以及 AI&HPC(16%)。下游客戶
為 AMD、Broadcom、Hisilicon、Samsung、Continental 以及 Delphi 等，主要
競爭對手為 Ibiden、Samsung、Shinko、欣興以及景碩。 
3Q26 營收達成率 125%，ABF 報價漲幅優於預期：南電 3Q26 營收 163.78 億元
(+20.6%QoQ，+49.3%YoY)，整體 3Q26 營收達成率 125%優於預期，另一方面
日前公布的 8 月自結稅後淨利亦優於預期 3 個百分點，暗示 ABF 報價漲幅優於預
期，研究部將2H26 報價漲幅由原先的 5-10%上調至 10-20%，研究部上調 3Q26
毛利率至 30.4%，稅後淨利 36.60 億元(+61.8%QoQ，+405.0%YoY)，單季稅後
EPS 上調至 5.66 元(原估 3.31 元)。 
ABF 供給吃緊延續，4Q26 營收 QoQ+13.3%：展望 4Q26，研究部認為 ABF 在
AI Server 高速運算、異質整合以及 Agentic AI 帶動 CPU 相關需求拉升下，供給
吃緊態勢延續，預期報價仍具 10-20%漲幅空間，上調單季營收預估值至 185.53
億元 (+13.3%QoQ ， +66.2%YoY)，毛利率 36.3% ， 稅 後 淨 利49.82 億元
(+36.1%QoQ，+314.6%YoY)，單季稅後 EPS 上調至 7.71 元(原估 3.88 元)。 
2H26 ABF 報價優於預期，2026 年獲利上修 48.7%：展望 2026 年，研究部原預
期 ABF 2H26 單季漲幅 5-10%，惟受惠需求優於預期，報價漲幅上調 5-10 個百
分點，搭配整體產業稼動率維持高檔，相關族群獲利具上修空間，考量南電產品主
要走短約更易反映供需，另一方面公司 ABF 營收占比亦達 60%以上，在報價優於
預期時對獲利反映更加敏感，研究部上調全年營收至 596.83 億元(+48.6%YoY)，
毛利率由 20.4%上調至 28.2%，稅後淨利 122.13 億元(+527.3%YoY)，稅後 EPS 
18.90 元(原估 12.71 元)。

Agentic AI 助攻，ABF 需求更具延續性：過去 ABF 需求主要聚焦 AI Server 所帶
動的 GPU 及 ASIC 需求，隨著 Agentic AI 大量導入，LLM 於多工處理時 CPU 資
源耗用增加，同步帶動相關需求提升，根據 AMD 於 2Q26 法說預期相關 CPU 產
值將由 2026 年的 600 億美金上升至 2030 年的 2000 億美金，研究部認為在 AI
產品週期由 GPU、ASIC 擴散至 CPU 下，ABF 需求將更具延續性，搭配指標大廠
Ibiden(4026 JP)於日前法說訊息，ABF 至 2028 年仍供給吃緊。 
ABF 供給吃緊可望延續 ， 南電2027-2028 年獲利翻倍上修 ：Nittobo 獨供的 Low 
CTE 玻纖布在 AI Server 用 ABF core layer 板厚增加 50%，造成產能供給吃緊，
連帶排擠 BT 載板中 AP 及 memory 相關供應，研究部預期即便 3Q27 新產能開
出仍不足支應目前 AI Server 殷切需求，ABF 供給吃緊態勢不變。研究部上修南電
2027-2028 年 獲 利 預 估 ，2027 年 營 收 上 修 至39.8% 來到 914.11 億元
(+53.2%YoY)，毛利率 48.3%，稅後淨利 329.08 億元(+169.5%YoY)，稅後 EPS
上修 135.2%至 50.93 元。2028 年營收上修至 1212.26 億元(+32.6%YoY)，毛利
率 53.0%，稅後淨利 494.41 億元(+50.2%YoY)，稅後 EPS 76.51 元(原估 42.03
元)，整體獲利趨勢仍呈高速成長。

## Document 190: 9月ＦＯＭＣ議事録：タカ派姿勢に滲む「データ依存」の慎重さ ～FRBは年内追加利上げを視野に入れるも、決定は今後の経済指標次第～

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月ＦＯＭＣ議事録：タカ派姿勢に滲む「データ依存」の慎重さ   ～FRBは年内追加利上げを視野に入れるも、決定は今後の経済指標次第～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

FED  Watching

2026 年 10 月 8 日

1/5 
9 月ＦＯＭＣ議事録：タカ派姿勢に滲む「データ依存」の慎重さ 
～FRB は年内追加利上げを視野に入れるも、決定は今後の経済指標次第～ 
 経済調査部 主任エコノミスト  桂畑 誠治  
（℡：050-5474-7493）

要旨  
・9 月FOMCでは全会一致（12 対 0）で 0.25%の利上げを決定。非投票メンバーを含む参加者 
全 19 人が利上げに賛成した。 
・経済・金利見通し（SEP）提出者の大半が年内（10 月または 12 月）の更なる利上げを見込 
んでおり、全体としてタカ派的トーンが際立つ。 
・インフレリスクは上振れ傾向にあるものの、中長期のインフレ予想は 2%目標水準に安定。 
FRBは毎会合のデータ次第で柔軟に判断する「オープンな姿勢」を維持している。 
・今後の見通しとしては、景気や労働市場が安定する中でコアCPIやコアPCEの鈍化が確認され 
れば、予防的な追加利上げが見送られる可能性がある（最終決定は 12 月FOMCまでのデータ次
第） 。

１． 9 月FOMC ：参加者 19 人全員 が利上げ に賛成  
ケビン・ウォーシュFRB議長の新体制下で初めての利上げが決定された 9 月FOMCの議事録が公表 
された。新体制では会合直後の声明文のテキスト量が大幅に削減されたほか、記者会見時間も短
縮され、直接的な情報発信量が絞り込まれている。そうした中で、後日公表される「議事録」の
情報量は引き続き高水準に維持されており、FRBの本音を推し量る最重要ツールとしての重みが増
している。ただし、公表がFOMC終了から 3 週間後となるため、情報の遅効性には注意が必要であ 
る。 
9 月の利上げ決定については、投票権のないメンバーも含めた参加者全員（19 人）が利上げに賛
成した。さらに、経済・金利予測（SEP：議長自身は今回も SEP を提出していない）で示されていた 
通り、SEP を提出した 18 人の参加者のうち大半が「年内（10 月 27-28 日または 12 月 15-16 日）に
更なる利上げが適切となる可能性が高い」と見解を示しており、インフレへの強い警戒感を示すタ 
カ派（金融引き締め寄り）なトーンが際立つものとなった。ただし、委員会として今後の政策判断 
は新たに入手されるデータに依存（Data-dependent）するスタンスを明確に維持している。

２．労働市場は完全雇用水準で安定、インフレには上振れリスク 
委員会は全会一致（12 対 0）で、FF金利誘導目標レンジを 0.25％ポイント引き上げ、3.75％〜

### Page 2

2/5 
4.00％とすることを決定した。労働市場が完全雇用に近い水準で安定している一方で、インフレ 
率が高止まりしていることが背景にある。 
インフレの判断では、参加者は共通して、 「インフレ率は高止まりしており、ここ数ヶ月でイン 
フレ抑制に向けた十分な進展は見られていない」と指摘した。 「原油や精製燃料製品の価格を押し 
上げた継続的な地政学的動向や、急増するAI関連投資がインフレ圧力に寄与している」との認識 
が示された。 
複数の参加者がコア財・コアサービス価格の上振れや、関税の影響に言及したほか、数名の参 
加者がPCE指数の算出手法改定による影響や、3 ヶ月年率換算のインフレ率が年後半にインフレを 
過小評価しやすい傾向に留意すべきだと警鐘を鳴らした。一方で参加者全員が、 「中長期のインフ 
レ予想に関する市場ベースおよび調査ベースの指標が、委員会の 2％目標と一致する水準を維持し 
ている」と判断しており、追加利上げを過度に急ぐ必要性は示されなかった。 
労働市場に関しては、参加者は概ね「労働市場の状況が安定している」と判断し、 「完全雇用に 
近い状態にある」と評価した。 「失業率が現在の水準付近にとどまり、労働市場の状況は引き続き 
安定する」と予想した。一方で、数名の参加者が低水準の採用・解雇率など流動性の低下を指摘 
し、一部の参加者がAI専門職における局所的な賃金上昇圧力に触れた。そのうえで、参加者は概
ね「労働市場に対する上振れリスクと下振れリスクはほぼ均衡している」と評価した。 
経済活動に関しても、参加者は一般に「堅調なペースで拡大している」と評価。 「地政学的動向 
による供給面での悪影響が強まったものの、旺盛な設備投資と底堅い個人消費が経済活動を支え 
ている」との判断が示された。なお、一部の参加者からは、AI関連投資が設備投資と潜在生産性 
を押し上げる一方で中期的に総需要が総供給を上回るリスクや、株高などを背景とする金融環境 
の緩和的状況、高所得層と低・中所得層における消費の二極化構造への懸念が指摘された。 
以上のように、参加者は共通して「労働市場が完全雇用に近く力強さの兆候も見られる一方で 
インフレ率は高止まりしており、経済活動が堅調なペースで拡大していること」を強調した。さ
らに、ほぼすべての参加者が、 「インフレリスクは上振れに傾いているものの、労働市場に対する 
リスクは減少し、現在はほぼ均衡している」と評価した。こうした経済見通しと変化するリスク 
バランスに基づき、全参加者（19 人）がFF金利誘導目標レンジの引き上げが適切であると判断し
た。

3．政策金利の水準感とリスク管理の姿勢 
今回の議論において注目されるのは、利上げの根拠と政策金利水準に対する見解の多様さであ 
る。多くの参加者は、予想以上の需要や供給面での悪影響によりインフレ率が持続的に目標を上 
回り続けることに対する保険（Insurance）を提供するため、誘導目標レンジを高めの軌道とする 
ことがリスク管理上の観点から賢明であると強調した。 
一方で、相当数の参加者はリスク管理上の観点からではなく、自身の標準的な見通し（Modal 
outlooks）に基づいて高めの目標軌道が必要であると見なした。さらに、数名の参加者は中立金 
利の推計値を引き上げたことに触れ、現在の政策金利は「緊縮的ではない、あるいは極めて緩や

かに緊縮的であるに過ぎない」とのタカ派的な認識を示した。これは、今後の引き締め継続を正
当化する材料となる。 
また、バランスシート政策に関しては、数名の参加者が国債市場の円滑な機能を認めつつも、 
将来の市場不全に備えたツールやコミュニケーションの強化を提案したことが確認された。

４．先行きに対する見通し 
今回の会合以降の金融政策の見通しに関して、大半の参加者（SEP提出者 18 人のうち）が年末 
までにFF金利誘導目標レンジを再度引き上げることが適切となる可能性が高いとの見方を示し 
た。ただし、全員が共通のスタンスとしてオープンな姿勢で各会合に臨み、今後の決定は新たに 
入手する情報と、それが経済見通しおよびリスクバランスに与える含意に依存することを強調、
毎会合ごとに判断されることを示し、政策の自由度を維持した。 
今回の議事録は全体として「年内追加利上げ」に前向きなタカ派的トーンが目立ったものの、 
インフレ予想の安定や「毎会合データ次第」というスタンスから、FRBが無理な連続利上げを焦っ 
ているわけではないことが示された。今後の金融政策の焦点を左右するのは、10 月・12 月FOMCま
でに発表される景気、労働市場や、コアCPI、コアPCEの動向である。景気や労働市場の安定が続 
くと予想されるもとで、インフレの鈍化傾向が確認される場合、予防的な追加利上げは見送られ 
る可能性がある（最終決定は今後のデータ次第） 。

## Document 193: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서_9ee69886

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서_9ee69886.pdf

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

SK하이닉스 (000660)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상  
3Q26 영업이익: 상반기와 다르다. 억제 요인들의 추세 반전 전환 
SK하이닉스의 3Q26 영업이익은 전분기 대비 32% 증가한 80.1조원으로 시장기대
치를 능가할 전망이다. 최근 원화강세 흐름 및 상반기 동사의 상대적 실적개선 속도 
열위에 3Q26 영업이익 추정치는 70조원까지 내려갔다 (컨센서스 74조원). 하지만 
이제 동사 출하량 및 판가의 탄력적 복원세가 경쟁사 보다 빠른 실적 개선을 이끌 전
망이다. 3Q26 DRAM과 NAND 출하량/판가 증감률은 각각 +13%/+22%, 
+4%/+15% 수준으로, 각 영업이익은 63조원/17조원으로 예상된다 (OPM 각각 
84%/69% 추정). 그간의 상대적 실적 열위는 이제 반전된다. 이는 1) 판가 전략에 
있어 중장기적 관점에서 고객 중심 유연성 극대화를 꾀했던 부분의 보상적 되돌림이 
발생하고, 2) 업계에서 제일 높은 HBM 판매 비중으로 인해 판가 상승률에 발목 잡혔
지만, 오히려 HBM4 본격 공급이 시작되며 이익기여가 본격화된다. 아울러 3) 모바
일 핵심고객과의 기성 계약이 산업 전반적 LTA 논의로 재검토되며 공급사에 크게 유
리한 조건으로 변화되어 가기 시작한다. 당사는 1Q27부터 모바일 메모리 판가 급등
을 전망하며 (그림1), 2026E/2027E 영업이익의 경우 264조원/454조원으로 상향조정한
다. 4Q26 내 시장 실적 추정치 상향조정 및 투자자 수급의 구조적 개선을 전망한다.

‘지능기술’ 시대, 수요의 가격 역탄력성 속 중장기 기회창출에 주목 
과거 B2C 사이클과 다르게, 금번 B2B 사이클은 수요의 가격 역탄력성을 보이고 있
다. AGI 도달을 위한 선착순 투자가 핵심이기 때문이다. 빅테크 중 낙오자가 발생하
기 전까지 경쟁적 대규모 투자는 지속된다. 최근 동사의 솔리다임 상장 추진보도에 
일부 우려가 제기되고 있지만, 투자자들은 그 이면의 중장기적 기회에 주목해야 한다. 
동사는 LTA 외에도, 다양한 파트너십을 추진하며 중장기 시장 예측력을 증가시키고 
있다. 솔리다임 상장 과정에서 유입되는 현금은 모회사 ‘SK AI co’의 지분교환 및 JV 
구축 등에 활용되리라 예상된다. 이는 중장기 수요를 ‘창출’시키는 개념이다.

순수한 주주가치 제고 목적 환원 재도출 예상. 적정주가 상향조정 
빠르게 증가한 현금흐름 중 ‘잉여’분은 주주가치 ‘회복’에 우선 배분돼야 함이 마땅하
며, 동사 역시 최근 공시를 통해 주주가치 제고와 주가의 저평가 인식을 드러냈다. 현
재 진행중인 40조원 자사주 매입 프로그램 종료 후 즉각적인 신규 환원 정책 도출이 
예상된다. 2027년 메모리 공급부족은 명확하다. 역사적으로 효율적 시장 내, 주가 조
정은 탄력적 복원으로 귀결되어왔다. 동사에 대한 투자의견 Buy를 유지하며, 적정주
가의 경우 320만원으로 상향한다 (26E/27E 평균 P/B 4.0배 적용). 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 12

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

## Document 199: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성전자 (005930)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정  
3Q26E 영업이익 106.1조원, 원화강세 불구 견조한 수준 기록 전망 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익은 106.1조원으로 낮아진 시장 기대치 (104조원)를 소폭 
능가할 전망이다. 해당 분기동안 급격한 원화강세로 인해 실적 측면의 비우호적 환경
이 조성되었지만, 동사는 강건한 판매 정책에 기반해 메모리 판가가 산업 평균을 재
차 능가하며 견조한 실적 개선이 가능했다. 
동사 메모리 사업부의 3Q26 영업이익은 109조원으로 예상되며, 이중 DRAM과 
NAND는 각각 80조원 (OPM 78.5%) 과 29조원 (OPM 68.9%) 을 차지할 전망이다. 
DRAM과 NAND 출하량은 기존 예상치를 소폭 하회하는 +3.4%/+4.2% QoQ을 기
록하겠지만, 판가가 무려 +20.1%/19.0% QoQ 상승하며 실적 개선을 이끌겠다. 전
분기에 이어 ‘DS 특별경영성과급’ 충당금이 반영되며 손익에 일부 영향을 미치겠지
만, 동사 특유의 ‘수익성 극대화’ 판가 정책이 산업 우위 구도를 조성한다. 한편 LSI/
파운드리는 영업손실이 1.5조원을 기록하며 적자가 지속될 전망이다. 가동률 개선에 
기반한 사업 회복이 진행중이지만, 테일러팹 가동과 함께 비용 요인이 지속 출회할 
전망이다. 한편 반도체 외 기타 사업부의 험난한 실적 추이는 지속될 전망이다. SDC 
1.1조원 영업이익을 제외하고, DX (세트) 부문의 경우 MX -1.9조원, DA/VD -7,400
억원으로 영업적자 확대되리라 예상된다. 당사가 지난해 말부터 주장했듯, 세트 사업
은 메모리 등 부품원가 비중이 급격히 상승하는 가운데 향후 ‘판가 인상 → 판매량 
축소’의 ‘내구재화’ 변모 속 적자 최소화 노력만이 불가피할 전망이다.

1Q27 일부 모바일 DRAM 판가 +50% 폭등 예상. 적극적 매수 권고 
AGI를 향한 경쟁적이며 구조적 서버 수요 증가 불구, 스마트폰 중심의 B2C 응용처
는 유일한 판가저항처로 남아있다. 하지만 당사의 채널체크에 따르면, 메모리 업체들
의 지속적인 생산 전환 (LPDDR → 서버 DRAM 및 HBM)에 기반해 주요 고객 향 
모바일 메모리 판가는 무려 1Q27부터 50% 이상 QoQ 상향될 전망이다. 주요 고객
은 내년 20주년 플래그십 스마트폰을 제작하는 상황에서 판가 폭등을 감내해야하는 
상황이다. 최근 메모리 사이클 개선 속도에 대한 막연하며 진부한 우려가 존재하나, 
향후 1~2개월간 메모리 업체들에 대한 2027년 실적 추정치 상향과 함께 ‘성장·저평
가·주주환원 매력’이 동시에 발생할 전망이다. 동사의 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 기
존의 562조원에서 632조원으로 13% 상향조정하며, 시장 눈높이 역시 지속 상향되
리라 예상한다. 한편 배당 중심으로 발표됐던 동사의 주주환원의 경우, 모든 주주에게 
더 실효적인 ‘자사주 매입·소각’ 방안이 병행되는 방향으로 변모하리라 예상된다. 투
자의견 Buy와 적정주가 50만원을 유지한다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 7

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 500,000원 
현재주가 (10.6) 272,000원 
상승여력 83.8%

## Document 232: AI交易连遭暴击！避险升温，芯片指数收跌近7%，AI产业链全线溃败前、散户抢筹创纪录

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI交易连遭暴击！避险升温，芯片指数收跌近7%，AI产业链全线溃败前、散户抢筹创纪录.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

沙特遭袭推动布油涨破 110 美元，通胀与避险压⼒加剧，或抬⾼未来投资成本、压缩估值空
间； Meta 资本开⽀上调、 8250 亿未来⽀出承诺引发融资担忧，股价收跌 7% ； Lam
Research 供应受限，半导体设备股增⻓预期遭重新审视。芯⽚设计、制造和设备和存储股
全线承压，收盘 Lam Research 跌超 10% 、 AMD 跌超 8% 、闪迪跌 8.5% 。光通信及 AI 基础
设施类股重挫，收盘 Coherent 跌超 10% 、 CoreWeave 跌 10% 、戴尔跌近 7% 。
摘要：
10 ⽉ 22 ⽇周四，中东局势升级引发的避险情绪席卷美股， AI 产业链成为重灾区。市场重新审视
AI 巨额资本开⽀的回报前景，加码 AI 投资的 Meta 也遭抛售。散⼾此前⼀周集中买⼊ AI ⾏业股，
令热⻔科技股在⻛险偏好骤降时更容易受冲击。同时，避险资⾦涌⼊美国国债， 10 年期美债收
益率下破 5% ，显⽰市场压⼒已从 AI 概念股扩散⾄宏观利率市场。
芯⽚股成为重灾区。周三反弹约 2% 的费城半导体指数（ SOX ）和半导体⾏业 ETF SOXX 周四收

跌将近 7% 。个股中，英伟达（ NVDA ）收跌 4.4% ， AMD （ AMD ）收跌 8.1% ， Lam Research
（ LRCX ）收跌约 11.8% ，科磊（ KLAC ）收跌近 9.6% ；存储芯⽚、光通信和 AI 云计算板块普遍
重挫。
Meta （ META ）盘中⼀度跌超 8% ，该司信⽤⻛险指标五年期信⽤违约互换（ CDS ）价格升⾄历
史⾼位，凸显投资者对 AI 投资规模、未来融资需求及回报周期的担忧。
这轮抛售的核⼼，是市场开始重新评估 AI 投资热潮的成本、融资压⼒与回报周期。 Meta 第三季
度营收和利润均超过预期，却进⼀步上调全年资本开⽀指引，并披露未来⽀出承诺升⾄ 8250 亿
美元； Lam Research 则因供应限制问题引发市场对半导体设备企业增⻓兑现节奏的担忧。
与此同时，沙特遭袭推动布伦特原油涨破 110 美元 / 桶，能源成本和通胀⻛险重新受到关注。 AI 投
资回报的不确定性、产业链供应约束与地缘政治⻛险交织，推动投资者削减⾼估值科技资产敞

⼝，并转向相对安全的债券。
彭博指出，周四的市场表现并⾮单纯的 AI 交易逻辑逆转，更显著的特征是避险情绪主导的⻛险资
产抛售。盘中纳斯达克 100 指数⼀度跌 3% ，标普 500 指数跌超 1% ，⽽等权重标普 500 指数反⽽涨
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

0.2% ，显⽰抛售主要集中在科技巨头等⼤型成⻓股。与此同时，债券上涨、收益率下⾏，也反
映出投资者转向避险资产。
Themis Trading 合伙⼈兼股票交易联席主管 Joseph Saluzzi 表⽰： “ 感觉这就是⼀个避险交易
⽇，⽽科技股总是最先遭到打击。 ” 他还指出，债券上涨也印证了市场正在采取避险策略。投资者
担⼼， “ 中期选举后，伊朗战争可能升级，并且持续时间可能⽐预期更⻓。 ”
中东⻛险推⾼油价，避险交易冲击科技巨头
沙特遭袭加剧市场对中东冲突扩⼤的担忧，布伦特原油盘中升破每桶 110 美元。油价上涨重新点
燃通胀担忧，⽽美国中期选举已不⾜两周，能源价格上⾏可能进⼀步增加市场对通胀和政策前
景的顾虑。
在这⼀背景下，资⾦开始削减⻛险资产敞⼝，倾向转投相对安全的国债，科技股因估值较⾼、
对未来增⻓预期依赖更强⽽率先承压。美债价格上涨与美股下跌周四同步出现，进⼀步凸显当
天⾏情中的避险特征。因此，半导体指数的⼤幅下挫虽发⽣在 AI 产业链内部，但不能简单归结为
市场全⾯看空 AI 。

油价上涨不仅增加全球经济的成本压⼒，也可能推⾼通胀预期，令市场担忧美联储等央⾏降息
空间受到限制。与此同时， AI 基础设施建设本⾝需要⼤量资⾦、电⼒和设备，能源价格上涨与融
资成本压⼒可能进⼀步影响投资回报的测算。
对 AI 概念股⽽⾔，这构成双重压⼒：⼀⽅⾯，市场要求企业证明巨额投资能够兑现回报；另⼀⽅
⾯，油价上涨和潜在利率压⼒⼜可能抬⾼未来投资成本、压缩估值空间。
周四芯⽚股的普跌说明， AI 交易已进⼊更看重盈利兑现、资本效率和融资可持续性的阶段。强劲
需求仍然是⾏业的重要⽀撑，但仅凭需求增⻓已不⾜以让所有产业链公司持续获得估值溢价。
未来⼏周⼤型科技公司的财报、资本开⽀计划以及芯⽚设备企业对供应链和订单的展望，将成
为判断 AI 交易能否企稳的关键。
芯⽚⻰头普跌，设备股领跌产业链
半导体板块周四⼤幅回落，芯⽚设计、制造、设备和存储股⼏乎全线承压。到收盘，博通
（ AVGO ）跌 4.3% ， Marvell Technology （ MRVL ）跌 8.7% 、英特尔（ INTC ）跌 8% 、台积电美

股（ TSM ）跌 4.5% 。
半导体设备股跌幅更为突出。除了⾄少收跌近 10% 的 Lam Research 和科磊，应⽤材料（ AMAT ）
收跌 8.2% 。存储芯⽚股中，闪迪（ SNDK ）收跌 8.5% ， SK 海⼒⼠美股（ SKHY ）收跌 8.1% ，希
捷科技（ STX ）收跌 7.9% ，西部数据（ WDC ）收跌 6.4% ，美光科技（ MU ）收跌 6.3% 。

### Page 3

光 通 信 及 AI 基 础 设施 相 关 股 票 同 样 遭 到 抛 售 。 到收 盘 ， Coherent （ COHR ） 跌 12.5% 、
Lumentum （ LITE ）跌 11.5% ， Credo （ CRDO ）跌 10.7% 、康宁（ GLW ）跌 8.1% 。 AI 云计算服
务商 CoreWeave （ CRWV ）收跌约 10% ， Nebius （ NBIS ）收跌 7.2% 。到收盘，戴尔（ DELL ）
跌约 6.8% 、超微电脑（ SMCI ）跌 5.2% 、甲⻣⽂（ ORCL ）跌约 2.8% 。
跨产业链的普跌表明，市场担忧已从个别芯⽚公司的估值扩展⾄整个 AI 基础设施投资链条。芯⽚
设计商、设备供应商、存储⼚商、光通信企业和云服务商的增⻓预期，都与⼤型科技公司持续
投⼊ AI 基础设施的能⼒密切相关。⼀旦投资者开始质疑⽀出的回报速度，相关资产便可能同时受
到冲击。
Meta 资本开⽀上调， 8250 亿美元未来⽀出承诺引发融资担忧
Meta 成为周四 AI 交易承压的另⼀焦点。公司第三季度营收同⽐增⻓ 26% ⾄ 646 亿美元，超过市场

预期；每股收益（ EPS ）为 3.83 美元，也⾼于分析师预期的 3.57 美元。但强劲的当期业绩未能消

### Page 4

除市场对未来投资成本的担忧， Meta 股价午盘曾跌超 8% ，收跌约 7% 。
Meta 将 2026 年资本开⽀指引上调⾄ 1350 亿⾄ 1450 亿美元，并将此前指引区间的下限从 1300 亿
美元提⾼。公司披露，包含租赁、算⼒交易和能源协议等在内的未来⽀出承诺已升⾄ 8250 亿美
元。
市场关注点正在从 AI 业务能否增⻓，转向增⻓需要多少资本、何时能够产⽣⾜够回报。 Meta
CEO 扎克伯格表⽰，公司投资正在取得成果，核⼼业务依然强劲， AI 助⼿ Muse 等产品也显⽰出
增⻓潜⼒。但公司同时承认，部分新 AI 投资的回报周期将⻓于核⼼业务。
Meta 的信⽤⻛险指标也随之恶化。彭博数据显⽰， Meta 五年期 CDS 价格盘中⼀度上升约 8.9 个
基点⾄ 119.3 个基点附近，创历史新⾼。 CDS 价格上涨意味着投资者对相关债务违约⻛险的对冲
成本上升，反映市场对公司不断扩张的 AI 投⼊及潜在融资需求更加谨慎。
Meta 还宣布向现有股东分配⼀类新的⽆投票权股票，未来可⽤于融资、员⼯薪酬和潜在收购。

该安排进⼀步引发市场对公司如何为 AI 扩张筹资的关注。
接下来，⾕歌⺟公司 Alphabet 、微软和亚⻢逊将陆续公布季度业绩。这些⼤型科技公司的财报
不仅将检验 AI 需求是否持续，也将揭⽰资本开⽀是否继续加速，以及企业能否逐步证明投⼊正在
转化为收⼊、利润和现⾦流。对市场⽽⾔， AI 交易的关键已不只是需求增⻓有多快，还包括⽀出
规模是否可持续。
Lam Research 供应受限，设备股增⻓预期遭重新审视
Lam Research 成为半导体设备板块的领跌者之⼀。公司公布季度业绩后，股价早盘就已跌超
10% ，到收盘跌近 12% 。公司对下⼀阶段业绩的展望以及供应限制问题，令投资者对⾏业增⻓
预期更加谨慎。

### Page 5

据报道，财报公布后， Lam Research 管理层表⽰，如果没有近期的供应限制，公司给出的业绩
指引本可以更⾼。供应约束意味着，即使 AI 相关芯⽚需求旺盛，设备企业也未必能够按预期速度
交付产品、确认收⼊。这让市场开始重新评估强劲订单能否及时转化为实际业绩。
半导体设备企业的估值⾼度依赖晶圆⼚扩产和先进制程投资。此前， AI 数据中⼼建设推动芯⽚需
求增⻓，带动晶圆⼚增加产能，并⽀撑设备企业订单与股价上涨。但设备交付受到限制，或客
⼾扩产计划出现调整，都可能影响未来⼏个季度的收⼊兑现节奏。
应⽤材料、科磊等同业股同步⼤跌，表明投资者并未将问题局限于 Lam Research ⼀家企业。设
备板块此前受益于 AI 资本开⽀预期，股价对未来增⻓的敏感度较⾼；当市场开始担忧供给瓶颈、
资本开⽀节奏和估值⽔平时，相关股票也容易出现放⼤式回调。
不过，供应限制并不等于 AI 芯⽚需求已经转弱。当前更值得关注的是，设备⼚商能否缓解交付瓶
颈，以及⼤型科技公司的数据中⼼投资能否持续⽀撑晶圆⼚扩产。后续财报和管理层指引，将

帮助市场判断本轮调整究竟是估值和仓位的修正，还是 AI 产业链增⻓预期开始发⽣变化。
散⼾抛售前⼀周集中抢筹 AI 股，热⻔交易⾯临更⼤压⼒
彭博援引摩根⼤通策略师 Arun Jain 的数据称，在周四暴跌之前的⼀周，即上周四⾄本周三，个
⼈投资者明显增加了对半导体公司、提供新⼀代云计算服务的 “ 新云 ” （ neocloud ）企业以及存储
设备公司的买⼊，同时对美股 “ 科技七巨头 ” 的买⼊⼒度有所减弱。 Jain 在周三给客⼾的报告中指
出，单只科技股的散⼾买⼊规模升⾄ 7 ⽉市场动量交易开始⽡解以来的最⾼⽔平。
散⼾对科技 ETF 的需求更加突出。相关 ETF ⼀周净流⼊规模达到有记录以来第⼆⾼⽔平，部分原
因是投资者在上周后半段趁跌买⼊。上周五，科技 ETF 录得有史以来最⼤单⽇资⾦流⼊，推动当
周买⼊规模达到历史平均⽔平的 2.1 个标准差。 VanEck 半导体 ETF （ SMH ）也是吸引⼤量资⾦的
基⾦之⼀。
散⼾买⼊最集中的个股包括闪迪、台积电和 Nebius ，⽽这三只股票周四均⾄少跌 4% 。 Nebius 此

前在社交媒体投资论坛上受到关注； SpaceX 股票则在周⼆前也位列散⼾最⻘睐的个股之列，但
公司发布积极财报、股价周⼆上涨 7.8% 后，散⼾于周三转⽽卖出。

### Page 6

⻛险提⽰及免责条款

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.

4 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;
Consumer Discretionary TR USD; Industrials TR USD; Real Estate TR USD; Communication Services TR USD; Materials  TR USD; Financials TR USD; Consumer Staples TR USD; Utilities TR USD;

Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Earnings above all 
• Higher oil prices are in a tussle with stronger growth. We expect strong growth 
to help the global economy weather inflation risks, allowing equities to focus on the 
strength of earnings growth. Oil prices remain the key risk to monitor 
• Maintain a preference for equities over bonds and cash.  Strong earnings 
growth means we would use the recent pause to add exposure to our preferred 
markets and themes. The US and Asia ex-Japan remain our preferred regions 
• Take advantage of higher bond yields via credit risk but cap duration. The 
risk of higher bond yields looks most pressing in ultra -long maturities, but credit 
quality remains strong. We are Overweight EM USD bonds 
Hits from the 1990s 
Rising bond yields have been a key market theme.  Since mid -year, 10 -year 
government bond yields have risen approximately 40-80bps across the US, Japan and 
Euro area. Against this backdrop, gold has delivered high single -digit returns, while 
global equities have delivered low single-digit returns. 
Higher oil prices are in a tussle with robust growth data . The lack of progress 
towards a negotiated settlement in the US -Iran conflict and rising fears of shipping 
restrictions extending to the Bab al-Mandab Strait have meant that oil prices are once 
again testing the USD 100/bbl level. These worries have been exacerbated by 
increasing worries about refined product (eg. diesel, jet fuel, etc) supply. Despite these 
concerns, US growth data has held up remarkably well, as illustrated by the latest PMI 
and retail sales data. This is underpinned by AI investment growth.  
Real, rather than nominal, energy prices are key.  A chart of inflation -adjusted 
energy prices, though, illustrates why the global economy has managed to hold up 
despite these risks. Oil prices in real (inflation-adjusted) terms are closer to long term 
averages rather than at true ‘oil shock’ levels. This, together with the global economy’s 
lower oil intensity relative to history, is why we expect  growth and inflation to avoid 
worst-case scenarios as long as nominal oil prices avoid an excursion into the USD 
120-150/bbl range.

The Fed is likely to hike twice more by mid -2027. The 
acceleration in AI-driven investment growth means we believe 
the chances of a shift from our core soft-landing to no-landing 
economic scenario have gone up. This, combined with still -
high inflation readings mean we now expect the Fed to hike 
rates twice more by mid -2027. In contrast, the BoJ and ECB 
are expected to hike thrice and once more respectively. 
Buy-the-dip in equities 
1990s rate hikes a better parallel then 2022 . There is 
understandably some concern about whether rising central 
bank policy rates, and rising bond yields across maturities, will 
present a challenge to equity markets. However, we believe 
rate hikes in the 1990s are a better historical parallel, when 
central banks were hiking rates into strong economic and 
earnings growth, resulting in equity markets maintaining their 
pace of gains. The 2022 hiking cycle, in contrast, represented 
an inflation surprise, which we believe is not the case today. 
Strong earnings growth overwhelms other factors today. 
Still-strong economic and earnings growth means that we 
remain constructive on the outlook for equities and other risky 
assets going into year -end and beyond. The pause over the 
last couple of months was likely driven by a combination or 
rising bond yields , September seasonality and excessive 
investor optimism around mid-year.  
However, earnings expectations for global equities remain 
very strong (especially in our preferred US and Asia ex-Japan 
regional markets). We believe these earnings remain 
sufficiently robust to outweigh the drag from higher bond 
yields. Therefore, w e remain Overweight equities relative to 
bonds and cash, though we believe it is prudent to ensure the 
size of the equities Overweight is not excessive. Within Asia, 
we also trim China equities to a core holding, leaving Taiwan 
as our only regional Overwei ght, given the ongoing focus on 
AI investment-driven markets for now. 
Bumps remain part of the journey. As we laid out in our mid-
year outlook, the second half of this year is expected to be 
characterised by positive, but bumpy returns as a result of the 
factors laid out in the previous section, but also US mid -term 
election risks. The second half has thus far played out as 
expected given sufficient bumps, but still robust earnings 
growth. We would not be surprised by some volatility around 
US mid-term elections, but any further pullbacks or pauses 
would be opportunit ies to raise exposure given strong 
earnings growth is expected to ultimately dominate. 
Prefer credit over duration 
Corporate credit quality remains strong . Aggregate 
indicators of credit quality suggest credit quality remains 
strong across both Investment Grade and High Yield bonds 
across most regions. US IG bond yields face some upward 
pressure from significant hyperscaler supply, but supply -
driven spread widening tends to be relatively short -lived in 
nature and should not signal significant credit quality worries. 
Longer-maturity bonds remain at risk of further volatility. 
In contrast, we continue to be concerned that the volatility in 
very long maturity bonds could extend. Continued inflation 
and debt worries mean it is possible investors continue to 
demand a greater ‘term premium’ (ie. a premium for holding 
bonds with very  long maturities). We continue to believe 
limiting bond duration to 3-7 years remains appropriate.  
We continue to prefer  corporate and EM over government 
bonds. This implies earning a higher yield by taking on more 
credit risk (via corporate or EM bonds) rather than adding 
more duration risk. Within bonds, we remain Overweight EM 
USD bonds and view IG and HY bonds as core holdings. 
Modest USD weakness; gold to grind higher 
USD bounce led by pricing of Fed hikes . However, we 
believe Fed hikes are now excessively priced. At the same 
time, we see room for non-US rates to rise further, especially 
in Japan where the undervalued JPY is likely to strengthen. 
This is why modest USD weakness is likely to resume. 
Gold to continue grinding higher. While the move higher is 
likely to be characteri sed by two -steps-forward-one-step-
back, we believe an ultimately softer USD and continued EM 
central bank demand should result in continued gains in the 
precious metal over both the next 3- and 12-month periods.

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): We have slightly reduced the probability of a soft landing from 50% by raising 
the odds of a ‘no -landing’. The global economy has  picked up steam this summer, powered by AI investments in the US and 
Asia. This is reflected in robust upgrades to corporate earnings estimates  and PMIs. Fiscal easing in Germany and Japan is 
supporting growth. Meanwhile, inflation has picked up amid higher energy prices due to a resurgen t Middle East conflict. As a 
result, the Fed turned hawkish, hiking rates for the first time since 2023. It was joined by the ECB and BoJ – the first time all 
three major central banks hiked rates in the same month. We expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the 
BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, disinflation should return by next year as energy and tariff effects fade, enabling 
the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. Meanwhile, China is likely to accelerate fiscal spending to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability): We raised the probability from 25% amid an acceleration in the AI-driven investment 
growth. If oil prices ease, US tax cuts, an AI -fuelled stock market boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan could boost 
“animal spirits” further. A global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also spur global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): This tail risk scenario includes 15% chance of a stagflation unfolding if geopolitical conflicts 
worsen and oil stays above USD 120/bbl. We also assign a 10% chance to a recession, potentially caused by a more rapid pace 
of rate hikes to quell inflation, a stock market downturn negating the wealth effect, or a bond selloff due to fiscal concerns.

Our policy rate outlook*; oil price and US, Euro area, German near-term, long-term inflation

Policy rates watch  
Fed turns hawkish to build credibility as oil reignites 
inflation; likely to hike twice more by June 2027. The US 
economy appears resilient, with consensus estimates for 
2026 GDP clustering around 2.1% for both 2026 and 2027. 
The main engine is the AI investment boom and hyperscaler 
datacentre buildout, reinforced by easy financial conditions 
and healthy private-sector balance sheets. The robust outlook 
was reflected in August’s PMI data, with both manufacturing 
and service sector confidence beating expectations. 
The labour market has picked up  this year , with average 
monthly payrolls of 80 ,000 vs. 10 ,000 in 2025 . The 
unemployment rate at 4.1%  suggests full employment, and 
the workweek lengthened in August. Offsetting vulnerabilities 
include a low savings rate, fading fiscal support, soft real  
personal spending and oil prices close to USD 100/bbl. 
Inflation remains a challenge. Core PCE inflation has run 
above 3% every month since March, and the hawkish case 
rests on supercore inflation remaining around 3 %, raising 
concerns about energy cost pass-through. Nevertheless, 
August's firm inflation print came from airfares, hotels and a 
record jump in wireless plans  (which is idiosyncratic) , while 
shelter cooled and core goods rose just 0. 7% y/y. Core 
consumer inflation at 2. 4% y/y is the lowest since 2021, and 
BEA methodology changes in end-September should 
mechanically shave 0.2-0.3pp off historical core PCE inflation. 
Following the unanimous Fed vote to hike in September, and 
still elevated inflation, we pencil in one more rate hike this 
year, followed by another 25bps hike to 4.5% by June 2027. 
This pace is slower than market pricing of another c. 90bps of 
hikes in the next 12 months. However, we expect disinflation 
to resume next year as the impact of oil prices and tariffs fade. 
This should create space for the Fed to cut rates by Q3 2027.

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8 
ECB to hike once more: Euro area activity has proved more 
resilient than expected. The consensus 2026 growth estimate 
has been revised up to 0.9%, from 0.5% as recently as July, 
with ECB staff projections upgraded to  1.4% for 2027 and 
1.5% for 2028. Manufacturing PMIs have improved to 52.7 
regionally and services PMI to 53.0, helped by German fiscal 
delivery and falling household savings rates. A softer Q3 looks 
weather-driven, due to heatwaves and low Rhine water levels, 
though higher energy prices and real yields still cap demand. 
Headline inflation hit a near three -year high of 3.3% y/y in 
August and may peak around 3.4 -3.6% in Q4 2026 before 
easing through 2027. Underlying pressures look calmer: core 
inflation drifted down to 2.4%, services eased, and negotiated 
wage growth slowed to 2.4% y/y in Q2, down from a peak of 
5.6% in 2024 . Long -term inflation expectations remain 
anchored with no evidence of second -round effects. Inflation 
risks are two -sided – oil above USD 100/bbl and gas above 
EUR 70/MWh are fuelling near -term inflation, which in turn 
should hurt demand, driving inflation below 2% by H2 2027. 
The ECB raised its deposit rate to 2.50% in September, the 
top of neutral estimates. We see scope for one more rate hike 
to 2.75% by year -end before a pause in H1 2027. As such 
pricing of 95bps more tightening toward an above-3% terminal 
rate appears too hawkish. We expect easing of energy-driven 
price pressures to allow for ECB rate cuts by Q3 2027. 
China to boost fiscal spending to support growth. China 
is entering a stabilisation phase in which production is firming, 
but the recovery remains narrow. The official manufacturing 
PMI rose to 49.8 in August from 49.2, with new orders, export 
orders and production back in expansionary territory, while 
export growth accelerated to 25.0% y/y. Momentum, however, 
is concentrated in high -tech and export manufacturing: retail 
sales, fixed-asset investment and credit growth remain soft, 
and the property downturn has yet to bottom. Full -year 2026 
growth should land near 4.5 –4.6%, broadly in line with the 
official 4.5–5% target, as domestic demand stays weak. 
Reflation is under way, but shallow and cost-driven rather than 
demand-led. Headline consumer inflation edged up to 0.8% 
y/y in August and producer inflation to 3.8%, lifted by higher 
commodity and energy prices and by official efforts to curb 
excessive price-cutting. Full-year inflation is expected around 
0.8–1.0%, against roughly zero in 2025, with producer 
inflation swinging from −2.6% to about +2.5%. The persistent 
supply-demand imbalance should cap the reflation impulse. 
Policy settings remain supportive, but with no rush to 
stimulate. Fiscal policy takes the primary role via faster 
spending and utilisation of bond proceeds, with broad fiscal 
spending potentially accelerating in H2 versus a sharp 
contraction in H1, and the augmented fiscal deficit widening 
modestly. Monetary policy plays a complementary role: a 
25bps cut in bank reserve requirements is likely, while the 
policy rate should stay unchanged. Slow public financing 
raises the likelihood of further policy fine-tuning this autumn. 
BoJ to hike once a quarter until Q2 2027. Japan's economy 
is proving more resilient than expected. The consensus 2026 
growth estimate has been revised up over the past month to 
0.8% from 0.6%. Exports rose 19% y/y in August, a twelfth 
consecutive y/y growth, while core machinery and machine 
tool orders indicate higher borrowing costs have yet to dent 
corporate investment appetite. Real wages climbed 2.4% y/y 
in July, the strongest since May 2021. The weak link is the 
household sector: spending continued to contract y/y, leaving 
an unusual two -speed economy in which external and 
corporate demand carry growth while domestic demand lags. 
Underlying inflation sits close to the 2% target, with pressure 
building upstream rather than from demand. Energy and 
commodity prices have risen sharply and earlier yen 
weakness has amplified import costs, with producer inflation 
running well ahead of CPI . The 10-year breakeven inflation 
has risen close to May’s record high of 2.3% amid growing risk 
of pass-through from producer to consumer prices.  
Bank of Japan Governor Ueda, after raising rates by 25bps to 
1.25% in September, alluded to rising inflation pressures, 
stating the policy environment has shifted to a “new phase”. 
He added that the BoJ must now act to prevent inflation from 
overshooting, rather than simply trying to reach the 2% target. 
September’s BoJ decision came in a 7-2 split vote, with two 
members nominated by Prime Minister Takaichi calling for a 
hold. However, Ueda’s post -decision comments lead us to 
believe that the BoJ board will be comfortable with a 25bps 
rate hike every quarter until Q2 2027, taking the rate to 2%.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

Our view

Foundation: Fixed income assets are a  core holding (Neutral) in our portfolios. Within the asset class, we maintain an 
Overweight stance on EM USD government bonds and an Underweight stance on DM government bonds.  
The Fed’s decision to hike rates in the past week confirms our expectations of a central bank willing to establish its independence 
against political pressure. The Fed's moderately hawkish pivot is helping restore its inflation-fighting credentials. We expect Fed 
to hike twice, one by year-end and another hike in H1 2027 to  bring the Fed funds rate to 4.5%. The price reaction in the US 
government bond yield curve le ads us to believe the short -to-middle part of the curve ha s factored in most of the rate 
expectations. We now prefer to add positions in the 3-7-year bond maturity bucket. As oil price moderates in H2 2027, we see 
room for the Fed to contemplate rate cuts. That would also enable 10-year yields to come down, alongside the front end of the 
curve. Meanwhile, solid corporate fundamentals and supportive technicals should keep yield premiums tight, even if the scope 
for further compression is limited. In EM, we continue to favour EM USD over local currency (LCY) government bonds. 
Opportunistic ideas: We are bullish on European bank additional tier-1 (AT1) bonds (contingent convertibles [CoCos1]; FX-
hedged), US TIPS, AAA-rated CLOs, US utilities corporate hybrids, broad US HY and AUD corporate bonds.

DM rates – Underweight 
We are Underweight DM government bonds. In the US, the 
Fed's policy pivot this month followed Chair Warsh's hawkish 
Jackson Hole speech and recent economic data prints, 
including a pick-up in job creation and inflation over the summer 
and a sharp rebound in oil prices due to the re-escalation of the 
Middle East conflict. Given the unanimous Fed decision to hike 
rates this week, we expect two more hikes by June 2027 on the 
back of robust growth amid strong AI investments and a 
balanced labour market, keeping inflation elevated.  
Over the next few months, we expect the 2-, 10- and 30-year 
US government bond yields to remain rangebound, with the 
10-year yield trading in the 5.00-5.25% range. The reasons 
for this include: 1) oil prices staying elevated and potentially 
rising further due to the fluid geopolitical situation in the Middle 
East; 2) AI capex plans showing no signs of slowing down, 
which continues to drive above -trend growth in the economy 
and AI -related heavy bond issuance. While short -covering 
trades could move bond yields lower temporarily as net-short 
positions in US government bond futures have reached 
extreme levels, we think such downshifts would be short-lived. 
By H2 2027, we expect oil prices to moderate and the 
inflationary effects from tariffs to fade, allowing the Fed to 
contemplate rate cuts. US 30 -year yields, on the other hand, 
are expected to remain elevated as the US fiscal deficit 
remains substantial next year. In addition, we do not think the 
so-called ‘Bessent put’  - expanding purchases of long -dated 
bonds, while skewing new issuance towards shorter 
maturities - will be effective as this does not address the 
fundamental issues  of rising debt levels . These factors are 
likely to keep the 'term premium' elevated. 
In the Euro area and Japan, the ECB and BoJ also hiked rates 
this month. It is the first time on record all three - Fed, ECB 
and BoJ - have hiked in the same month, as they respond 
primarily to a resurgence in energy -driven inflation amid tight 
job markets. BoJ is expected to continue hiking once every 
quarter till mid -2027 as we believe the bank  is behind the 
curve and will need to speed up the pace of rate hikes.

### Page 10

Global Market Outlook 10 
DM corporates – Core holding  
We maintain a Neutral allocation to both DM IG and DM HY 
corporate bonds, with a tactical bias to add exposure on 
episodic weakness, as carry continues to dominate total 
returns across developed market credit. Yield premiums have 
bounced but remain close to historical lows, while yields are 
still attractive, reinforcing a carry-led stance. This tight spread 
regime is anchored by robust issuer fundamentals, strong 
profitability, and resilient balance sheets, despite a visible 
moderation in rating upgrades over the past quarter.  
Fig. 12 AI issuance challenges IG technical backdrop 
AI issuance (total & share of total), US IG gross issuance

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
Technical supply dynamics are reshaping sector dispersion, 
particularly in the IG space, where heavy issuance related to 
AI continues to weigh on the long end of the curve. AI -driven 
supply is approaching USD 220bn for 2026  in US IG  and 
accounting for an increasingly significant share of gross 
issuance and crowding out other long-dated maturity bonds. 
We maintain a preference for selectivity at the long end, 
favouring the front to belly of the curve ( 3-7 years maturity) 
where risk -adjusted carry remains most durable. Issuance -
driven concessions in sectors with strong capital expenditure 
cycles (notably technology, capital goods, and basic 
industries) present opportunities for investors who can 
stomach near term volatility, especially when fund flows have 
remained robust: so far this year, high grade funds have seen 
cumulative inflows of ~8% of AUM. 
Fig. 13 DM IG spreads have bounced lately, but remain 
near historical lows 
Bloomberg Global Aggregate Corp Index, option-adjusted 
spread (OAS)

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
As an income proposition, DM HY continues to be well 
supported, with all-in yields in the mid -7% range and default 
rate still below trend for US HY in 2026. The shorter duration 
profile of HY, combined with its lower sensitivity to term 
premium and rate volatility, makes it an attractive sleeve in the 
current phase of the cycle. Despite experiencing net outflow 
YTD, HY demand has recovered from a net outflow position 
in the past 1-2 months, underscoring broad investor appetite 
for carry strategies even as spreads remain tight. We remain 
attentive to evolving rating migration trends and sector -
specific supply pressures, particularly as upgrades slow and 
new issuance clusters around key theme s. However, we  
continue to see HY bonds as core holding allocations driven 
by healthy issuer fundamentals and stable credit metrics. 
Fig. 14 DM HY spreads are near historical lows as well 
Bloomberg Global HY Aggregate Corporate Index, OAS

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM government bonds – Overweight USD, 
core holding for local currency (LCY) bonds 
We re tain an  Overweight position in EM USD government 
bonds and retain EM LCY government bonds as a Core 
holding, reflecting a balanced perspective across both 
regional performance and currency dynamics. In EM USD 
sovereign debt, periods of spread widening have been brief  
and shallow, with attractive carry drawing sustained demand 
amid resilient global growth and stable external conditions. 
Issuance patterns remain front -loaded across many regions, 
reinforcing supportive technicals; relatively light investor 
positioning con tributes to contained risk of unwinding and 
limits market impact during episodes of moderate outflow. 
Regional nuances are increasingly notable. Latin America’s 
sovereign debt has tended to outperform, supported by more 
proactive monetary policy responses and firmer external 
balances. In Asia, higher -rated issuers offer valuable buffer 
during bouts of glob al rate volatility, while select frontier 
exposures within Africa and EMEA remain more sensitive to 
liquidity shifts and changes in risk appetite. Index composition 
also shapes resilience: the EM USD benchmark is less 
exposed to oil price shocks as non -oil importers represent a 
large share of exposure compared to the LCY universe. These 
features are particularly important as spreads across EM USD 
sovereigns hover near historically tight levels, leaving limited 
room for error. We expect returns to be primarily driven by 
carry rather than further spread compression.

### Page 12

Global Market Outlook 12

Our view

We are Overweight global equities as we expect solid earnings growth to drive the market higher . The wave of central 
bank rate hikes in September represent a near-term headwind to equity valuations, but these have been largely priced into 
markets by the rise in bond yields year-to-date. We expect earnings growth to offset valuation headwinds. We are Overweight 
US and AxJ equities, where AI adoption continues to drive growth in the technology and adjacent sectors. 
Within AxJ, we remain Overweight Taiwan, with good visibility on AI-driven growth. We lower China to a Core holding, 
as the valuation re-rating potential appears delayed. Within AxJ, we maintain core allocations to South Korea and India, and we 
are Underweight on ASEAN equities. 
We maintain a Core allocation to Japan, as nominal growth supports broad earnings revision, and a Core allocation to Europe 
ex-UK, which is enjoying an earnings inflexion. We remain Underweight UK equities, which lacks exposure to growth sectors.
